Erratum (Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4, Proposition 2.15). As printed, the shifted weighting is `(ϑ + dη)_e = ϑ_e + η_{h(e)} - η_{t(e)}`, with the `i`-strands moved right by `η_i`. With the paper's walls `x_b - x_a = ϑ_e` (`a` of colour `h(e)`, `b` of colour `t(e)`), moving the strands by `η` changes `x_b - x_a` by `η_{t(e)} - η_{h(e)}`, so the walls are preserved exactly for `ϑ'_e = ϑ_e + η_{t(e)} - η_{h(e)}`. For example, with `ϑ = 0`, `η_{h(e)} = 1`, `η_{t(e)} = 0` and `x_b - x_a = 1`, the printed weighting gives the moved point the wall value `-1` instead of `1`. The proof in the paper (the ghost moves right by `η_{t(e)}`) uses the corrected sign. Formalized: `WeightedKLR.weightedKLRShiftEquiv` with the corrected sign; the printed sign is the same statement with the strands moved by `-η`. For the author: in Proposition 2.15, the coboundary should read `ϑ_e + η_{t(e)} - η_{h(e)}` (or the strands should move left by `η_i`); the proof is unaffected.

Erratum (Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4, §2.1, degree function). As printed, a dot on an `i`-strand has degree `⟨α_i, α_i⟩`, a crossing of strands of colours `i, j` degree `-δ_{ij}⟨α_i, α_i⟩`, and a crossing of a strand of colour `i` with a ghost of colour `j` degree `-½⟨α_i, α_j⟩(1 - δ_{ij})`. Reading the pairing as `⟨α_i, α_i⟩ = d_i` and `⟨α_i, α_j⟩ = -d_{h(e)} c_e` for a single edge, this makes every relation homogeneous; we use twice these degrees to avoid half-integers. It fails in three cases: (a) with several edges between `i` and `j` (of different weights), a ghost crossing of the edge `e` must have degree `½ d_{h(e)} c_e` for its own edge, since the ghost bigon of `e` multiplies by `Q_e` alone; (b) for a loop `e` of nonzero weight at `i`, the factor `1 - δ_{ij}` gives an `i`-strand crossing its own ghost degree `0`, while the bigon multiplies by `Q_e`, of degree `d_i c_e > 0`; (c) for a loop of weight `0` at `i`, the same-colour bigon relation, which involves `P_e`, forces a crossing of two `i`-strands to have the degree of `P_i`, not `-⟨α_i, α_i⟩`. (The pairing defined in §2.4 includes loop terms, which does not repair (b) or (c).) Corrected degree function: as in `WeightedKLR.WeightedGraph.Standing.gradingDatum` (dot `2d_i`, a step across a wall of `e` degree `d_{h(e)} c_e`, a same-colour crossing `-2d_i`, or `2d_i(c_i - 1)`, twice the degree of `P_e`, at a loop of weight `0`), halved for Webster's normalization. Formalized: `WeightedKLR.GradingDatum.gradedAlgebra` with this datum. For the author: in §2.1, the degree of a ghost crossing should be taken edge by edge (`½ d_{h(e)} c_e` for the ghost of the edge `e`, including loops of nonzero weight), and at a loop of weight `0` the crossing of two `i`-strands should have the degree of `P_e`.

Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2, §2.2, (2.2a–b), bubble degrees. For region weight n, the bubble labels in (2.2a) are −n−1−j and n−1−j. Negative-degree vanishing requires j>0, as printed in v3 (2.3a); v2 prints j<0. In (2.2b) the upper bound j+n+1 gives an empty sum when n=j=−2, although the right side is the identity. The bubble-series inversion stated in the text gives upper bound j−n+1. With d=j+2 the two normalized indices are a and d−a, where 0≤a≤d. Version 3 retains the upper bound in (2.3b). This affects the literal corner comparison: with one fundamental red and one downward black in sl₂ over ℚ, the exterior weight is −1, so at j=−2 the printed interval [0,−2] annihilates the boundary identity. The ordinary block is ℚ[x]/(x) ≅ ℚ, precluding an injective comparison (`DoubleDiagram.printedBubble_no_injection_sl2`). The signed presentation uses the repaired nonpositive degrees and convolution in `DoubleDiagram.bubble_eq_of_le` and `bubbleGrassmannian`. [v2, p. 9](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1309.3796v2#page=9) · [v3, p. 9](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1309.3796v3#page=9)

Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v3, §2.2, (2.2a), (2.3a), (2.5b), (2.6e).
Take disconnected colours i,j with Qᵢⱼ = Qⱼᵢ = 2 over ℚ, one red of
coroot coordinates (1,1), and one downward j to its right. Compose the
vertical mirror of the first cup pitchfork with the second cap pitchfork.
Both relations put the factor 2 on the downward-crossing side. The
downward and mixed bigons each have coefficient 2. The bare i-bubble
on either side of j has degree zero since aᵢⱼ = 0. Thus the composite
gives 8 id = 2 id. The ordinary one-black algebra is ℚ[x]/(x), hence
`DoubleDiagram.printedPitchfork_no_injection` rules out an injection
even with the corrected inverse-bubble bound. If c and d are the cup
and cap coefficients on the indicated sides, this example requires
c d = 1 to preserve that identity. Over any field the same necessary condition
holds for equal nonzero bigon coefficients and unit bubble closures
(`DoubleDiagram.pitchforkCoefficients_mul_eq_one`). A normalization for all colours
and orientations remains open. [Webster v3, Definition 2.4](https://arxiv.org/html/1309.3796v3#S2.SS2)


Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2, §4.5, violation and the red/up crossing. The expression κ(0) > 1 before Definition 4.27 uses an index outside the domain in Definition 4.23. The accompanying geometric condition is that the first strand is black, of either orientation (`DoubleBoundary.IsViolated`). With at least one red this is κ(1) > 0. With no red it means the word is nonempty. The two red/up inverse identities used in the proof of Theorem 4.29 appear in (4.4e); the citation there is to (4.4d), a three-strand relation (`DoubleDiagram.redUpCrossingIso`).

Erratum (Webster, arXiv:1309.3796v2, §4.7, Lemma 4.35). For insertion just before the red strands in gap j, increase κ(m) when κ(m) ≥ j. For deletion of black strand j+1, decrease κ(m) when κ(m) > j. The printed conditions are κ(m) ≤ j and κ(m) ≤ j+1. A single fundamental red followed by one black strand already gives a printed deletion coordinate of −1. The coroot coordinate n for the commutator is evaluated immediately before that lowering, at µ(j+1) + αᵢ for its colour i. Passing an i-strand lowers this coordinate by two (`FirstWeight.blackBlockPairing_cons_self`). In the example the input has coordinate +1 and the printed µ(j+1) gives −1. On the highest vector h, EFh = h ≠ 0 and FEh = 0. For n < 0 the summand on the FE side has graded multiplicity [−n]ᵢ. See `Stendhal.ProjectiveCommutator.printed_deletion_out_of_range`, `fundamental_commutator_weights` and `fundamental_commutator_on_highest`.

The quotient and tensor product targets have a separate [roadmap](docs/WeightedKLR/tensor-product-roadmap.md).

Theorem 2.19, proof, antipode recursion. With the tensor operator `id ⊗ S`, the summand containing `S(x)` has first weight zero. The displayed exclusion of second weight zero retains this summand. Apply multiplication after `id ⊗ S` and exclude first weight zero. Equivalently, in positive weight start with `−x` and subtract the products over splits with both weights positive. On a single strand this gives `S(x) = −x`. See `GradingDatum.antipodeK0`.

Theorem 2.19, quantum-group map: the supporting polynomial conditions
`KLR.IsRouquierQ` and `WeightedGraph.Standing.isRouquierQ`, the Cartan datum
`Standing.cartanDatum`, and the leading-coefficient Serre identities
`KLR.Rouquier.serre_A`, `serre_B`, `serre_C` hold over arbitrary commutative coefficients.
Graded principal-projective induction (`GradingDatum.indK0_ofIdempotent`) and homogeneous
sequence-corner maps (`GradingDatum.spacedEntryMap_mem_klrGrade`) provide the weighted
transfer. The graded Serre relation is `KLR.Rouquier.K0_serre_window`, over every commutative
ring with Rouquier parameters and the canonical Cartan grading. The multiplicative
quantum-group map is `WeightedGraph.Standing.quantumGroupMap`, for loop-free graphs
over any field under the standing and (†) hypotheses; `rationalQuantumGroupMap`
is its scalar-extended counterpart. The map to integral `K_0` uses the integral divided-power form; the generic
`ℚ(v)` form requires scalar extension of the target. Proposition 2.14 is implemented as
a matrix loading-family corner, not necessarily a corner of the chamber algebra.
The generation clause of Proposition 2.18 concerns the ordinary KLR subcategory and does not imply surjectivity
onto weighted `K_0`; the middle-chamber example in the roadmap rules out that claim.

Erratum (Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4, Theorem 2.19, ground ring). §2.1 fixes an arbitrary commutative ring `k`, and Theorem 2.19 asserts that `K = ⊕_ν K_0(W_ν)` is a twisted bialgebra over `ℤ[q, q^{-1}]` with coproduct given by restriction. Restriction lands in `⊕ K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗_k W_{ν''})`, and the comparison map `K_0(W_{ν'}) ⊗_{ℤ[q^±]} K_0(W_{ν''}) → K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗_k W_{ν''})` need not be an isomorphism. Already for `ν' = ν'' = 0` (where `W_0 = k`) it is the multiplication map `K_0(k) ⊗_ℤ K_0(k) → K_0(k)` with `q^±` adjoined, which is bijective only if `K_0(k) = ℤ` or `k = 0`. For `k = ℤ × ℤ` with idempotent `e`, the class `[ke] ⊗ [k(1 - e)]` is nonzero and maps to `0`, for every `Γ`, `ϑ` and `Q`; the degree-zero part of `K` is then `K_0(k)[q^±]` rather than `ℤ[q^±]`, so the unit and counit are not those of a bialgebra over `ℤ[q^±]`. Corrected statement: for `k` a field (the convention of Khovanov–Lauda I, which the proof follows), with the coproduct composed with the inverse of the comparison map; for weighted KLR algebras this inverse exists when every graded simple module has endomorphism ring `k` (for example over an algebraically closed field). The parts that hold over any `k` (associativity and unit of the product, coassociativity and the Mackey identity of restriction into `⊕ K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})`) are stated over any commutative ring. Formalized: the any-ring part of the product (associativity and unit of `[P][Q] = [Ind(P ⊠ Q)]` on ungraded `K_0`, `WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.indK0_assoc`, `indK0_one_left`, `indK0_one_right`) and coassociativity of restriction into `⊕ K_0(W_{ν'} ⊗ W_{ν''})` (`WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.resK0_coassoc`, under the hypotheses of `res_projective`); the coproduct into `K_0(W_{ν₁}) ⊗ K_0(W_{ν₂})` and its Mackey formula are formalized ungraded under the explicit hypothesis that the comparison map is bijective (`WeightedKLR.coproductK0`, `WeightedKLR.exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum`, not proved), and over a field on graded `K_0` without a comparison hypothesis for loop-free graphs (`WeightedKLR.GradingDatum.tensorK0Hyp`, `WeightedKLR.exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded`); the conditional version for graphs with loops is `exists_coproductK0_indK0_eq_sum_graded_of_tensorK0Hyp`; A0 proves `fdA`, `fdB`, `fdC`, and A proves `endA`, `endB` for loop-free graphs over any field with standing `Q` (`WeightedKLR.GradingDatum.tensorK0Hyp`); the graded coproduct formula needs no comparison hypothesis in that scope (proof and scope in `docs/WeightedKLR/thm-2-19-roadmap.md`); roadmap row §2.4, Theorem 2.19 above. For the author: in Theorem 2.19 the ground ring should be a field (and graded simple modules absolutely irreducible); over a general commutative ring, `K_0` of `W_{ν'} ⊗_k W_{ν''}` differs from `K_0(W_{ν'}) ⊗ K_0(W_{ν''})`, already for `k = ℤ × ℤ` and `ν' = ν'' = 0`.

Notation note (Webster, arXiv:1209.2463v4, proof of Theorem 2.19, grading shift). The proof states that the subquotients of the Mackey filtration of `Res ∘ Ind` are shifted by the inner product `-⟨μ, μ'⟩`. The shift is the degree of the diagram exchanging a block of strands of dimension vector `μ` with one of dimension vector `μ'`, and this degree is `-(μ · μ')` for the symmetric form `i · j = ⟨i, j⟩ + ⟨j, i⟩`, as in KL I Prop. 2.18: the exchange crosses each hyperplane joining the two blocks exactly once, and a strand of colour `i` and one of colour `j` contribute the walls of the edges between `i` and `j` in both directions (`WeightedKLR.Juxtapose.segDeg_chamber_swapChamber`, `WeightedKLR.GradingDatum.pairDeg_comm`). So the printed shift is correct when `⟨-, -⟩` is read as the symmetric pairing of roots of §2.1 (where `⟨α_i, α_i⟩` is the degree of a dot), and wrong for the non-symmetric form `⟨j, i⟩ = d_i δ_{ij} - ∑_{e : j → i} d_i c_e` defined just before in §2.4. For one edge `e : 0 → 1` with `d = c_e = 1`, exchanging one strand of each colour crosses one wall of degree `1` in both orders, while the §2.4 form gives `⟨α_1, α_0⟩ = 0`. The twisted product on `K_0` in the statement of Theorem 2.19 uses the symmetric form, so only the reading of the proof is affected. For the authors: in the proof of Theorem 2.19, the shift `-⟨μ, μ'⟩` of the Mackey subquotients refers to the symmetric pairing of §2.1, not to the form `⟨j, i⟩` of §2.4; writing `-(μ · μ')` would avoid the clash.

### Lusztig's algebra `f` (Lusztig, *Introduction to quantum groups*, Ch. 1; KL I §3.1)

For a symmetric Cartan datum (`QuantumGroup.CartanDatum`, including the simply-laced datum of a
graph, `CartanDatum.ofGraph`) over a commutative ring with a unit `v`:

| Source | Result | Declarations |
|---|---|---|
| Lusztig 1.2.1–1.2.2 | the free algebra `'f`, the twisted algebra `'f ⊗ 'f`, and `r : 'f → 'f ⊗ 'f` | `QuantumGroup.PreF`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.TwSq`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.r`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.tensorEquiv` |
| Lusztig 1.2.3; KL I Prop. 3.3 | existence, uniqueness and symmetry of the bilinear form | `QuantumGroup.PreF.existsUnique_form`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.form_symm` |
| Lusztig 1.2.5 | `f = 'f / radical`, with nondegenerate form | `QuantumGroup.PreF.F`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.formF_nondegenerate` |
| Lusztig 1.4.3 | the quantum Serre relations hold in `f` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.serre`; simply-laced: `QuantumGroup.KL.serre_comm`, `QuantumGroup.KL.serre_cubic` |
| Lusztig 1.4.7 | divided powers and the integral form `_A f` | `QuantumGroup.PreF.dpow`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.Af` |
| Lusztig 1.4.1 | `θ_i^{(a)} θ_i^{(b)} = [a+b choose a]_i θ_i^{(a+b)}` | `QuantumGroup.PreF.dpow_mul_dpow`, `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.dpowF_mul_dpowF` |
| Lusztig 1.4.3 | divided-power form of the Serre relations; simply-laced: `θ_iθ_jθ_i = θ_i^{(2)}θ_j + θ_jθ_i^{(2)}` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.serre_divided`, `QuantumGroup.KL.serre_divided` |
| Lusztig 1.2.2, 1.4.2 | coassociativity of `r` on `'f`; `r` descends to `f`; `r(θ_i^{(a)})` | `QuantumGroup.PreF.coassoc`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.rbar`, `QuantumGroup.PreF.r_dpow` |
| Lusztig 1.4.4 | `(θ_i^{(a)}, θ_i^{(a)}) = ∏_{s=1}^a (1 − v_i^{−2s})^{−1}` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.form_dpow_dpow`, `QuantumGroup.KL.form_dpow_dpow` |
| Lusztig 1.2.12, 1.4.7 | the bar involution on `f`, preserving `_A f` | `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.barF`, `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.barF_mem_Af` |

The converse inclusion (the radical is generated by the Serre relations; Lusztig 33.1.3, used
in KL I §3) is `QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.quantumGabberKac` (proved via lie-lean, see above). In the
list of generators of the ideal in KL I §3.1, `θ_iθ_j − θ_iθ_j` should read `θ_iθ_j − θ_jθ_i`.
In the right-module half of KL I Prop. 2.13, the summands should be the right projectives
`_{…i^{(2)}j…}P ⊕ _{…ji^{(2)}…}P`. In KL I §2.2, `L(i^m)` is nonzero in degrees `0, 2, …, m(m−1)`
(with `deg x = 2`), not `m(m−1)/2`.

### Graded modules and Grothendieck groups

Generic infrastructure over a `ℤ`-graded algebra (`Categorification.Graded`): graded modules via
Mathlib's `DirectSum.Decomposition` and `SetLike.GradedSMul`, grading shifts `M{a}` (shifted up
by `a`, as in KL I §2.5), graded dimension `gdim` in `ℤ((q))` (additive on short exact
sequences, `gdim (M{a}) = q^a gdim M`), the Grothendieck group `K0` of finitely generated graded
projective modules as a `ℤ[q,q⁻¹]`-module (with `[A e] = q^{-d}[A e']` for idempotents
equivalent via elements of degrees `d`, `-d`, and additivity on orthogonal idempotents), and
`G0` of finite-dimensional graded modules. For `R(ν)`: `P_i = R(ν) 1_i` and characters
`ch(M)` (`KLR.GradingDatum.projP`, `KLR.GradingDatum.ch`). "Graded projective" is taken to mean
projective as a module and graded. The forms on `K_0` are defined through `HOM` spaces:
`Graded.K0.homForm` (`([P], [Q]) = gdim HOM(P, Q)`, antilinear in the first variable) and
`Graded.K0.pform` (`(x, y) = homForm x̄ y` with the bar involution `[P] ↦ [P̄]`,
`P̄ = HOM(P, A)^ψ`; bilinear and symmetric). For `P` finitely generated graded projective and any
graded `N`, `m ⊗ n ↦ (f ↦ ψ(f(m)) n)` is a degree-preserving isomorphism
`P^ψ ⊗_A N ≅ HOM(P̄, N)` (`Graded.twistTensorGradedEquiv`, `Graded.gdim_twistTensor`), so the
paper's form `gdim(P^ψ ⊗_{R(ν)} Q)` is `pform` on all of `K_0`
(`Graded.K0.pform_of_eq_gdim_twistTensor`, `Graded.K0.eq_pform_of_gdim_twistTensor`), equivalently
`homForm x y = gdim(x̄^ψ ⊗ y)`, and the paper's pairing `gdim(P^ψ ⊗_{R(ν)} M)` with `G_0` is
`(x, y) ↦ pairing x̄ y` (`Graded.eq_pairing_bar_of_gdim_twistTensor`). On the self-dual classes
`[P_i]` all of these agree.

### The map `γ` (KL I §1, §3.1)

For the simply-laced datum of a graph (`KLR.KLGamma`): the classes `[P_{i_1^{(a_1)}⋯}]` in
`K_0(R)` with `[P_i][P_j] = [P_{ij}]`, `[P_î] = i![P_i]` and the Serre relations; the algebra map
`'f → K_0(R)`, `θ_i ↦ [P_i]` (over `ℤ[q,q⁻¹]`), which kills the Serre relations; its base change
`γ : f → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` (with `q ↦ v⁻¹`) sending divided-power monomials to the corresponding
`[P_{i_1^{(a_1)}⋯}]` (`KLR.KLGamma.gammaF`, `gammaF_dpowMono`), and the integral version on `_A f`
(`gammaInt`). Descending from `'f/(Serre)` to `f` uses the Gabber–Kac theorem, which enters as an
explicit argument `QuantumGroup.PreF.GabberKac` (discharged by `QuantumGroup.KL.gabberKac`); the integral map into `K_0(R)` uses that
`K_0(R) → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` is injective, which holds because `K_0(R)` is free (see below).

**KL I Prop. 3.4** (injectivity): Lusztig's form on words is
`(θ_{a_1}⋯θ_{a_m}, θ_{b_1}⋯θ_{b_m}) = ∏_x c_{a_x} · ∑_{w : b∘w = a} v^{∑_{(x,y) ∈ inv(w)} a_y·a_x}`
(`QuantumGroup.PreF.form_wordFn`), which matches `gdim(1_j R(ν) 1_i)` under `q ↦ v⁻¹`
(`KLR.KLGamma.lsCast_homForm_projP`); hence the kernel of `'f → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` is the radical of the
form, unconditionally (`KLR.KLGamma.ker_gammaQ_le_radical`), and `γ` is injective on `f` and on
`_A f` (`KLR.KLGamma.gammaF_injective`, `gammaA_injective`, `gammaInt_injective`, under the
hypotheses above). `γ` intertwines Lusztig's `r` with `[Res]` (`KLR.KLGamma.coprod_gammaZ`,
`KLR.KLGamma.map_gammaF_rbar`; see the twisted bialgebra paragraph at the top and
`KLR.KLGamma.theorem_1_1_bialgebra`).

### Modified quantum groups (Khovanov–Lauda III, arXiv:0807.3250v1, §2.1)

For a root datum over a field with `q` not a root of unity: Lusztig's `U̇` as the direct sum of its
blocks `U̇1_λ` (`QuantumGroup.UDot.UD`), its integral form, the involutions `ψ` and `ω`, the
bilinear pairing of KL III Prop. 2.2 (existence and uniqueness, `QuantumGroup.UDot.prop_2_2`,
`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_2`) and the semilinear form with the properties of Prop. 2.4
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_4`). Nondegeneracy of both forms (Prop. 2.5) for any Cartan datum,
under the Gabber–Kac inclusion of the radical of the form on `'f` into the Serre ideal
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_5`); since `U̇` is defined by the Serre presentation, this
hypothesis is also necessary (`QuantumGroup.UDot.B_posF_radical`). It holds for every Cartan datum
(`QuantumGroup.CartanDatum.quantumGabberKac`), so Prop. 2.5 is unconditional
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_5_unconditional`; rank one directly:
`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.prop_2_5_sl2`). The proof
identifies the bilinear form with a tensor-product form on a model of `U̇1_λ` in `'f ⊗ 'f`
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.B_eq_ndP`), avoiding canonical bases. Theorem 2.7: the forms `(E_𝐢1_λ, E_𝐣1_μ) = ⟨E_𝐢1_λ, E_𝐣1_μ⟩` are the sum over
`(𝐢, 𝐣)`-pairings of `q` to the degree of a minimal diagram times the bubble contributions
(`QuantumGroup.UDot.KL3.thm_2_7`, any Cartan datum). In the example following Theorem 2.7, the
value printed as `1/(1-q_i)^2 · 1/(1-q_j)` should be `1/(1-q_i^2)^2 · 1/(1-q_j^2)`.

### Krull–Schmidt, projective covers, and the Grothendieck groups (KL I §2.5)

Over a field, for a `ℤ`-graded algebra with finite-dimensional graded pieces vanishing in
sufficiently negative degrees (`Categorification.Graded`): graded projectivity of projective graded
modules, Fitting's lemma, indecomposable graded projectives have local degree-0 endomorphism rings and
unique tops, every graded simple has a unique projective cover, `K_0` is a free `ℤ[q,q⁻¹]`-module
with basis the indecomposables up to shift (`Graded.K0.indecBasis`), `G_0` is free with basis the
graded simples (`Graded.G0.topBasis`), and the pairing `([P],[M]) = gdim HOM(P, M)` pairs the two
bases diagonally (`Graded.pairing_indecBasis_topBasis`). For `R(ν)`: `KLR.GradingDatum.k0Basis`,
`KLR.GradingDatum.g0Basis`, finitely many indecomposables (at most `(m!)²`), and consequently
`K_0(R) → ℚ(v) ⊗ K_0(R)` is injective (`KLR.KLGamma.toK0Q_injective`), so the integral `γ` needs only
the Gabber–Kac hypothesis (`KLR.KLGamma.gammaInt'`). Krull–Schmidt also holds at the level of
modules: every graded f.g. projective is a finite sum of shifted indecomposables, uniquely up to
permutation, and `[P] = [P']` iff `P ≅ P'` (`Graded.GProj.exists_iso_sumList`,
`Graded.GProj.perm_of_iso_of_iso`, `Graded.K0.of_eq_of_iff`).

### Symmetric group combinatorics

Words in adjacent transpositions of `Fin m`: Coxeter length and inversions
(`TypeA.length_eq_invCount`), a normal form (`TypeA.braidEquiv_canWord_or_hasRepeat`), and
Matsumoto's theorem for `S_m` (`TypeA.braidEquiv_of_isReduced`), and factorisation through
minimal coset representatives of `S_n × S_{n'}` with additivity of length
(`TypeA.parabolicEquiv`, `TypeA.length_blockPerm_mul`).

### Symmetric polynomials

`Categorification.symmetricBasis`: over any commutative ring, `k[x_0, …, x_{n-1}]` is a free
module of rank `n!` over the symmetric polynomials, with basis the monomials `x^u`,
`u_a ≤ a` (`finrank_symmetric`); more generally, free of rank `|G|` over the invariants of a
label-preserving group `G` (`Categorification.finrank_labelInvariants`).

### Divided differences

`Categorification.ddiff`: divided difference operators on `MvPolynomial` over any commutative
ring, with the nilHecke relations (`ddiff_spec`, `ddiff_ddiff`, `ddiff_mul`, `ddiff_braid`).

## Building

Requires [elan](https://github.com/leanprover/elan). Toolchain
`leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1` and Mathlib `v4.34.1` are pinned.
The default branch is `master`. The project dependencies
`string-diagrams-lean` and `lie-lean` are pinned to Git revisions in `lakefile.lean` and
`lake-manifest.json`. Warnings are errors; the default build covers every
`Categorification` submodule.

```sh
lake exe cache get
lake build
```
